surrounding environment.\n  manifestation: When I go places now I look around for what might fall, where the exits are, and whether the floor or steps look steady before I can sit easy.\n- key: S080\n Oh, goodness no. Hopeless? No, Doctor, I certainly wouldn't use a word like that.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I suppose that's actually the main reason my wife wanted me to come see you, Doctor. My sleep just hasn't been quite right lately. 

I don't want to make a big fuss about it, you know, because a lot of folks my age don't sleep like they used to. But... well, I do lie there for a good long while before I can drift off. And then, yes, I tend to wake up. Sometimes it's just a little noise that startles me, or... or I'll have a bad dream, and then my eyes just pop open. After that, I just can't seem to settle back down and get comfortable again. 

I try not to toss and turn too much so I don't bother my wife. I just lie there quietly. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? Just wait it out? I hope it's just a normal part of getting older, Doctor. It's nothing too serious, is it?

I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you're resting or trying to fall asleep, do you ever feel an uncomfortable urge to move your legs, or a strange feeling in your legs that only goes away when you move them?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, sir. I don't get anything like that. My legs stay put just fine when I'm resting. I mean, they might get a little stiff sometimes, just from being an old man, but no strange feelings or urges to kick them around. \n\nI try to keep them relaxed, you know? Just lay quiet and not make a fuss. I don't want to be one of those folks who can't sit still and bothers everyone around them. I hope that's the right answer? I'm just trying to give you the facts straight so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your sleep difficulties are affecting your mood, energy, or ability to enjoy your usual activities during the day?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say my mood is bad, Doctor. I really try to stay cheerful, you know? Keep a positive outlook. I don't want to be a grump to my wife or anything. \n\nI guess... maybe I don't have quite the same pep in my step for my little projects as I used to. Like I said, I haven't been down to the senior center, and I suppose I just haven't felt much like doing the things I usually enjoy. But I'm not down in the dumps or anything like that! I just... I like keeping things quiet right now. That's okay, isn't it? It's normal for a man my age to slow down a little bit, right? I just want to make sure I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you feel little pleasure or interest in doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to keep my chin up, Doctor. I really do. I certainly haven't given up on my little hobbies or anything like that. I still get out there and water the garden, and I tinker with my old radios some... I try to make sure I'm keeping my hands busy and staying productive, you know? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest with you, the good feeling I used to get from doing those things, it just... it doesn't come quite like it did. It's hard to explain. I go through the motions, but that little spark of joy just isn't really there right now. \n\nBut I still do them! I make sure I get up and do them. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I figure a fellow my age isn't going to be jumping for joy over every little thing anyway, right? I hope I'm handling it the right way. I'm doing okay, aren't I, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having little pleasure in life most days, not just when it comes to your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, not at all. I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most days. I really try to count my blessings, you know? I've got a good roof over my head, my wife is wonderful... there's a lot to be thankful for. I make a point to smile and greet the neighbors when I see them. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe things feel a little... quieter lately, like I mentioned with the hobbies. But I'm certainly not walking around with a raincloud over my head all day. I try to be pleasant to be around. Nobody likes a sourpuss, right? I'm doing alright by your book, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most of the time, even when you're not thinking about your sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no. Hopeless? No, Doctor, I certainly wouldn't use a word like that. \n\nLike I was just saying, I really try to look on the bright side of things. I don't sit around moping or feeling sad, I promise you that. I've got a good life. Even if I don't have a whole lot of fancy schooling or anything, I know enough to be grateful for what's in front of me. I just try to be a good husband and keep a smile on my face for the folks around me. \n\nThat's the best way to handle things, don't you think? Just keeping your chin up? I suppose that's actually the main reason my wife wanted me to come see you, Doctor. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I wouldn't say "distress," Doctor. That sounds like a very strong word. I'm still getting up and doing my basic routines, you know? Brushing my teeth, getting dressed... I'm not just lying in bed all day. 

But, I suppose... well, my wife has had to take over some of the chores around the house that I used to do. I just haven't had the energy, or maybe I'm just avoiding certain things. I feel terrible about it, really I do. I want to be a good help to her. 

And... I guess I haven't been going down to the senior center lately. Or seeing my friends for bridge. It's just easier to stay home where it's quiet. I suppose my days have gotten a little smaller. But that's okay for a man my age, right? It's normal to slow down a bit and not be so social all the time? I just don't want to be a burden on anyone, Doctor. I hope I'm still doing okay in your eyes.

Well, I wouldn't say "almost no activities," Doctor. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting in a dark room all day. I'm still trying my best to stay busy.

I'm just trying to take it easy so I don't cause any trouble. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you're resting or trying to fall asleep, do you ever feel an uncomfortable urge to move your legs, or a strange feeling in your legs that only goes away when you move them?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, sir. I don't get anything like that. My legs stay put just fine when I'm resting. I mean, they might get a little stiff sometimes, just from being an old man, but no strange feelings or urges to kick them around. \n\nI try to keep them relaxed, you know? I just want to make sure I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you feel little pleasure or interest in doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to keep my chin up, Doctor. I really do. I certainly haven't given up on my little hobbies or anything like that. I still get out there and water the garden, and I tinker with my old radios some... I try to make sure I'm keeping my hands busy and staying productive, you know? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest with you, the good feeling I used to get from doing those things, it just... it doesn't come quite like it did. I hope I'm handling it the right way. I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most days. I really try to count my blessings, you know? I've got a good roof over my head, my wife is wonderful... there's a lot to be thankful for. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I think I just mentioned this, Doctor, but I don't want to give you the wrong impression. I really am trying to keep up with things. I haven't just stopped caring about everything. 

I still make sure to get out there and water the garden, and I spend a little time tinkering with those old radios in the garage. I know it's good for me to stay active. It's just... well, like I said, that good feeling I used to get from it, the... the joy, I suppose... it just doesn't come like it did. 

I still watch a little television with my wife in the evenings, too. I try to be good company for her. It's just harder to feel that same warmth or happiness about it all. But I'm still making the effort, Doctor. That's what you're supposed to do, right? Just keep putting one foot in front of the other? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.

I just want to make sure I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you feel little pleasure or interest in doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to keep my chin up, Doctor. I really do. I certainly haven't given up on my little hobbies or anything like that. I still get out there and water the garden, and I tinker with my old radios some... I try to make sure I'm keeping my hands busy and staying productive, you know? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest with you, the good feeling I used to get from doing those things, it just... it doesn't come quite like it did. It's hard to explain. I go through the motions, but that little spark of joy just isn't really there right now. \n\nBut I still do them! I make sure I get up and do them. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I figure a fellow my age isn't going to be jumping for joy over every little thing anyway, right? I hope I'm handling it the right way. I'm doing okay, aren't I, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having little pleasure in life most days, not just when it comes to your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, not at all. I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most days. I really try to count my blessings, you know? I've got a good roof over my head, my wife is wonderful... there's a lot to be thankful for. I make a point to smile and greet the neighbors when I see them. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe things feel a little... quieter lately, like I mentioned with the hobbies. But I'm certainly not walking around with a raincloud over my head all day. I try to be pleasant to be around. Nobody likes a sourpuss, right? I just take things one day at a time, like a fellow should. I'm doing alright by your book, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most of the time, even when you're not thinking about your sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no. Hopeless? No, Doctor, I certainly wouldn't use a word like that. \n\nLike I was just saying, I really try to look on the bright side of things. I don't sit around moping or feeling sad, I promise you that. I've got a good life. Even if I don't have a whole lot of fancy schooling or anything, I know enough to be grateful for what's in front of me. I just try to be a good husband and keep a smile on my face for the folks around me. \n\nThat's the best way to handle things, don't you think? Just keeping your chin up? I hope I'm not giving you any cause for concern, Doctor. I really am trying my best to be a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up earlier than you'd like in the morning and find it hard to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose that's actually the main reason my wife wanted me to come see you, Doctor. My sleep just hasn't been quite right lately. \n\nI don't want to make a big fuss about it, you know, because a lot of folks my age don't sleep like they used to. But... well, I do lie there for a good long while before I can drift off. And then, yes, I tend to wake up. Sometimes it's just a little noise that startles me, or... or I'll have a bad dream, and then my eyes just pop open. After that, I just can't seem to settle back down and get comfortable again. \n\nI try not to toss and turn too much so I don't bother my wife. I just lie there quietly. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? Just wait it out? I hope it's just a normal part of getting older, Doctor. It's nothing too serious, is it?\n\nDoctor: Have your sleep problems caused you any significant distress or made it harder for you to function in your daily life, like in your relationships or ability to enjoy time with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say \"distress,\" Doctor. That sounds like a very strong word. I'm still getting up and doing my basic routines, you know? Brushing my teeth, getting dressed... I'm not just lying in bed all day. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, my wife has had to take over some of the chores around the house that I used to do. It's hard to explain. I go through the motions, but that little spark of joy just isn't really there right now. \n\nBut I still do them! I make sure I get up and do them. I make a point to smile and greet the neighbors when I see them. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe things feel a little... quieter lately, like I mentioned with the hobbies. But I'm certainly not walking around with a raincloud over my head all day. I try to be pleasant to be around. Nobody likes a sourpuss, right? I just take things one day at a time, like a fellow should. I'm doing alright by your book, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most of the time, even when you're not thinking about your sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no. Hopeless? No, Doctor, I certainly wouldn't use a word like that. \n\nLike I was just saying, I really try to look on the bright side of things. I don't sit around moping or feeling sad, I promise you that. I've got a good life. Even if I don't have a whole lot of fancy schooling or anything, I know enough to be grateful for what's in front of me. I just try to be a good husband and keep a smile on my face for the folks around me. \n\nThat's the best way to handle things, don't you think? Just keeping your chin up? I hope I'm not giving you any cause for concern, Doctor. I really am trying my best to be a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up earlier than you'd like in the morning and find it hard to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I just want to make sure I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you feel little pleasure or interest in doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to keep my chin up, Doctor. I really do. I certainly haven't given up on my little hobbies or anything like that. I still get out there and water the garden, and I tinker with my old radios some... I try to make sure I'm keeping my hands busy and staying productive, you know? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest with you, the good feeling I used to get from doing those things, it just... it doesn't come quite like it did. It's hard to explain. I go through the motions, but that little spark of joy just isn't really there right now. \n\nBut I still do them! I make sure I get up and do them. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I figure a fellow my age isn't going to be jumping for joy over every little thing anyway, right? I hope I'm handling it the right way. I'm doing okay, aren't I, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having little pleasure in life most days, not just when it comes to your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, not at all. I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most days. I really try to count my blessings, you know? I've got a good roof over my head, my wife is wonderful... there's a lot to be thankful for. I make a point to smile and greet the neighbors when I see them. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe things feel a little... quieter lately, like I mentioned with the hobbies. But I'm certainly not walking around with a raincloud over my head all day. I try to be pleasant to be around. Nobody likes a sourpuss, right? Sometimes it's just a little noise that startles me, or... or I'll have a bad dream, and then my eyes just pop open. After that, I just can't seem to settle back down and get comfortable again. \n\nI try not to toss and turn too much so I don't bother my wife. I just lie there quietly. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? Just wait it out? I hope it's just a normal part of getting older, Doctor. I'm not just lying in bed all day. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, my wife has had to take over some of the chores around the house that I used to do. I mean, they might get a little stiff sometimes, just from being an old man, but no strange feelings or urges to kick them around. \n\nI try to keep them relaxed, you know? Just lay quiet and not make a fuss. I don't want to be one of those folks who can't sit still and bothers everyone around them. I hope that's the right answer? I'm just trying to give you the facts straight so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your sleep difficulties are affecting your mood, energy, or ability to enjoy your usual activities during the day?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say my mood is bad, Doctor. I really try to stay cheerful, you know? Keep a positive outlook. I don't want to be a grump to my wife or anything. \n\nI guess... maybe I don't have quite the same pep in my step for my little projects as I used to. Like I said, I haven't been down to the senior center, and I suppose I just haven't felt much like doing the things I usually enjoy. But I'm not down in the dumps or anything like that! I just... I like keeping things quiet right now. That's okay, isn't it? It's normal for a man my age to slow down a little bit, right? I just want to make sure I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you feel little pleasure or interest in doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to keep my chin up, Doctor. I really do. I certainly haven't given up on my little hobbies or anything like that. I still get out there and water the garden, and I tinker with my old radios some... I try to make sure I'm keeping my hands busy and staying productive, you know? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest with you, the good feeling I used to get from doing those things, it just... it doesn't come quite like it did. It's hard to explain. I go through the motions, but that little spark of joy just isn't really there right now. \n\nBut I still do them! I make sure I get up and do them. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I figure a fellow my age isn't going to be jumping for joy over every little thing anyway, right? I hope I'm handling it the right way. I'm doing okay, aren't I, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having little pleasure in life most days, not just when it comes to your hobbies?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, not at all. I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most days. I really try to count my blessings, you know? I've got a good roof over my head, my wife is wonderful... there's a lot to be thankful for. I make a point to smile and greet the neighbors when I see them. \n\nI mean, sure, maybe things feel a little... quieter lately, like I mentioned with the hobbies. But I'm certainly not walking around with a raincloud over my head all day. I try to be pleasant to be around. Nobody likes a sourpuss, right? I just take things one day at a time, like a fellow should. I'm doing alright by your book, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most of the time, even when you're not thinking about your sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no. Hopeless? No, Doctor, I certainly wouldn't use a word like that. \n\nLike I was just saying, I really try to look on the bright side of things. I don't sit around moping or feeling sad, I promise you that. I've got a good life. Even if I don't have a whole lot of fancy schooling or anything, I know enough to be grateful for what's in front of me. I just try to be a good husband and keep a smile on my face for the folks around me. \n\nThat's the best way to handle things, don't you think? Just keeping your chin up? I hope I'm not giving you any cause for concern, Doctor. I really am trying my best to be a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up earlier than you'd like in the morning and find it hard to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose that's actually the main reason my wife wanted me to come see you, Doctor. My sleep just hasn't been quite right lately. \n\nI don't want to make a big fuss about it, you know, because a lot of folks my age don't sleep like they used to. But... well, I do lie there for a good long while before I can drift off. And then, yes, I tend to wake up. Sometimes it's just a little noise that startles me, or... or I'll have a bad dream, and then my eyes just pop open. After that, I just can't seem to settle back down and get comfortable again. \n\nI try not to toss and turn too much so I don't bother my wife. I just lie there quietly. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? Just wait it out? I hope it's just a normal part of getting older, Doctor. It's nothing too serious, is it?\n\nDoctor: Have your sleep problems caused you any significant distress or made it harder for you to function in your daily life, like in your relationships or ability to enjoy time with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say \"distress,\" Doctor. That sounds like a very strong word. I'm still getting up and doing my basic routines, you know? Brushing my teeth, getting dressed... I'm not just lying in bed all day. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, my wife has had to take over some of the chores around the house that I used to do. I just haven't had the energy, or maybe I'm just avoiding certain things. I feel terrible about it, really I do. I want to be a good help to her. \n\nAnd... I guess I haven't been going down to the senior center lately. Or seeing my friends for bridge. It's just easier to stay home where it's quiet. I suppose my days have gotten a little smaller. But that's okay for a man my age, right? It's normal to slow down a bit and not be so social all the time? I just don't want to be a burden on anyone, Doctor. I hope I'm still doing okay in your eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you take pleasure or interest in almost no activities these days, even the ones you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say \"almost no activities,\" Doctor. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting in a dark room all day. I'm still trying my best to stay busy. \n\nI still go out and water the garden in the mornings, and... and I tinker with my old radios out in the garage sometimes. I know how important it is to keep your mind sharp and your hands moving, especially at my age. \n\nIt's just... well, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, the good feeling I used to get from those things, the... the spark, I guess you'd call it... it just doesn't come quite like it did before. It feels a little more like a routine now than something I look forward to. But I'm still doing them! That counts for something, doesn't it? It's better to keep trying than to just give up, right, Doctor? I'm doing the right thing by keeping at it, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take pleasure or interest in anything these days, even simple things like seeing friends, watching TV, or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I think I just mentioned this, Doctor, but I don't want to give you the wrong impression. I really am trying to keep up with things. I haven't just stopped caring about everything. \n\nI still make sure to get out there and water the garden, and I spend a little time tinkering with those old radios in the garage. I know it's good for me to stay active. It's just... well, like I said, that good feeling I used to get from it, the... the joy, I suppose... it just doesn't come like it did. \n\nI still watch a little television with my wife in the evenings, too. I try to be good company for her. It's just harder to feel that same warmth or happiness about it all. But I'm still making the effort, Doctor. That's what you're supposed to do, right? Just keep putting one foot in front of the other? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like things are less enjoyable than they used to be, even when you're not thinking about your sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor... I'm still out there putting the work in, I really am, but that good feeling just doesn't come over me like it did in the past. The enjoyment is just sort of... faded, I suppose, no matter what time of day it is. \n\nI hope I'm answering your questions right, Doctor. I'm really trying my best to explain it the right way for you. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that nothing gives you pleasure or interest anymore, not even things you used to enjoy or simple everyday moments?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... Like I mentioned, I still make sure to get out there and water the garden, and I still tinker with my old radios in the garage when I can. The good feeling I used to get from it, that little spark of happiness... it just doesn't come like it did. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, no, sir. I... I still have a good bit of get-up-and-go, I promise you that. I know I'm getting up there in age, but I make sure to keep moving. I don't let myself just sit around feeling tired all day. I'm up early, doing my chores and keeping things tidy.
